# Code review: Glyphbound, elemental build-up (read-only)

You are the second-opinion reviewer for **Glyphbound**, a first-person RPG shooter in Unreal Engine 5.9.0 (C++), made for a 4-day game jam. Do not modify any file. Do not build or run anything.

## Context
- Read `Design/ARCHITECTURE.md` first (short). The design is `Design/GDD.md` section 6 "Elemental build-up" (v0.22) and the v0.22 lines of section 18 "Numbers". The owner's original spec (Spanish) is `Prompts/feedback8_elemental_spec_owner.md`; the architect's design brief given to the implementer is `Prompts/feedback8_elemental_buildup.md`.
- The change under review is commit `99ee8f8` (`git show 99ee8f8 --stat`, then read the files). One AI implementer wrote it; the architect added one fix in `Combat/GBProjectile.cpp`. It builds, 34 tests pass, and a scripted smoke run passes in the Editor and in the packaged exe, so a missing include is NOT what we want from you.
- Code: `Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound/`. Data: `Glyphbound/Content/Data/*.json`.
- Engine source for checking APIs: `C:\Program Files\Epic Games\UE_5.9\Engine\Source`.

## What changed (review these areas)
1. **Meters** (`Combat/GBBuildUpMeter.*`): three phases (building, active, immune), decay after a delay, frame-rate independence, the event log that makes one event count once (biggest amount wins).
2. **Component** (`Combat/GBElementalStatusComponent.*`): config read from enemy stats with `Game.json` `ElementalDefault.*` fallbacks, reactions from enemy strings, tick only while busy (`SetComponentTickEnabled`, `TickInterval`), Ignite through `UGBStatusEffectComponent`, Shock discharge through `DealDamage` (re-entrancy: it damages its own owner from inside a damage call chain), Frost slow as a `MultiplyMin` modifier, cleanup on death / EndPlay / data reload.
3. **Damage path** (`Combat/GBCombatLibrary.*`, `Stats/GBAttributeComponent.cpp`, `Core/GBTypes.h`, `Core/GBStatNames.h`): `ApplyBuildUp` after a damaging hit, `IsWeakPointHit` split from `ApplyWeakPointBonus`, per-element damage taken, event ids.
4. **Sources** (`Spells/GBSpellTypes.cpp`, `Spells/GBSpell_Zone.cpp`, `Weapons/GBHeldItem.cpp`, `Items/GBInventoryComponent.cpp`, `Combat/GBProjectile.cpp`): one event id per shot / cast / blast / tick; shotgun pellets, piercing bolts, the fireball explosion, the rune blast (`Spells/GBSpell_Trap.cpp`), the flamethrower ticks (`Spells/GBSpell_Beam.cpp`), the ice floor (no damage, so it sends build-up directly).
5. **Enemy reactions** (`AI/GBEnemyCharacter.*`): stun / freeze lock that pauses the state machine, interrupt that cancels a windup. Look for: an enemy stuck forever, an attack landing after it was cancelled (a melee hit or a ranged projectile scheduled by a timer), movement continuing while locked, locks surviving death or era travel.
6. **Stats** (`Stats/GBStatsComponent.*`): the new `MultiplyMin` op; the ice floor's `Slow` effect now uses it; Deep Freeze and the 80% cap must be unchanged.
7. **Removed per-hit effects** (`Burn`, the old `Shock` slow, `Chill`): anything in code or data that still refers to them, or code that silently depended on them.
8. **Debug view and commands** (`UI/GBHUD.cpp` `gb.ShowElemental`, `Core/GBDevCommands.cpp` `gb.BuildUp` and the smoke step).

## What to report (real defects only, most severe first)
- Runtime bugs a player would hit: wrong build-up amounts (double counting, never counting), statuses that never end or never start, immunity not respected, slows stacking, Ignite damage that differs from the data, crashes on death / destroy / data reload.
- Contract breaks with existing systems: the flamethrower (mana, hold, interrupt), the ice floor (slow, Deep Freeze, QTE), weapon damage and head bonus, encounter progress on death.
- Data and code mismatches: a key in a JSON file that the code spells differently or never reads, and the reverse.
- Things that work in the Editor but break in a packaged build.
- Per-frame costs that grow with the number of enemies (the game will have hordes).
- Gameplay numbers hardcoded where data should decide.

Do not report: style, naming, formatting, missing comments, micro-optimizations, multiplayer issues (single player), or the number of tests.

## Output format
A numbered list, most severe first, at most 15 items. For each:
- `file:line` (or lines),
- what is wrong, in one or two sentences,
- why it matters when playing (what the player would see),
- the smallest fix you would make.
Finish with one short paragraph: the three things you would fix first, and anything you checked and found solid.

# Glyphbound: code architecture

> Written by the architect (Claude Code) on 2026-11-12. Source of truth for how the C++ is organized.
> Design lives in `Design/GDD.md`; this file says how the code implements it. Keep it short and current.

## Principles
1. **Data, not code.** Every gameplay number lives in `Glyphbound/Content/Data/*.json` and is read through `UGBDataSubsystem`. Code never hardcodes damage, costs, speeds, durations, sizes or timings. The JSON ships as loose files, so numbers can be tuned in a packaged build with a text editor (`gb.ReloadData` reloads while playing).
2. **One stat system.** A number is a *named stat*: base value from data, then modifiers. `UGBStatsComponent` evaluates `(Base + adds) * multipliers` (or an override). Skill-tree nodes, armor, status effects, air passives and the weapon enchant are all just groups of modifiers with a source name. Nothing else changes numbers.
3. **One damage path.** Everything that hurts goes through `UGBCombatLibrary::DealDamage(Target, FGBDamageInfo)`. Shields, resistances, teams, on-hit effects and kill credit hook in there once.
4. **Spells are data + a behavior.** A spell is a `FGBSpellDefinition` (params with `Base/Min/Max/ScaledBy`) plus the class of an `AGBSpellActor` behavior. The QTE result (power, reach) scales the params tagged for it. A new spell that fits an existing behavior is a JSON entry, no code.
5. **Shared pieces.** One projectile (`AGBProjectile`) for staff bolts, spell projectiles, enemy shots and grenades. One status-effect component for slows, burns, potions and buffs. One interact interface for portals, chests, pickups and the seal.
6. **Text in git.** No binary assets are required to run: input actions, the test arena, placeholder shapes and placeholder pixel sprites are all built in C++ from text. `Content/` is ignored by git except `Content/Data/`.
7. **No GAS.** Unreal's Gameplay Ability System was considered and rejected for the jam: it needs a lot of boilerplate and asset setup, and the game's needs (costs, cooldowns, timed effects, stat upgrades) are covered by principles 2-4 in a few hundred lines we fully control.
8. **PIE is human-only.** Agents build, run the pure-logic tests and package; a person plays.

## Module layout (`Glyphbound/Source/Glyphbound`, one runtime module, prefix `GB`)
Includes are relative to the module root: `#include "Stats/GBStatsComponent.h"`.

| Folder | What | Key types |
|---|---|---|
| `Core/` | Shared enums, stat names, placeholders (plain shapes and pixel-textured structures), game mode, dev commands | `GBTypes.h`, `GBStatNames.h`, `GBPlaceholder.h` (`SetupTexturedShape`), `AGBGameMode`, `UGBGameInstance`, `GBDevCommands.cpp` (`gb.Smoke`) |
| `Data/` | Definitions and the JSON loader | `GBDataTypes.h`, `UGBDataSubsystem` |
| `Stats/` | Stats + modifiers, health/mana | `UGBStatsComponent`, `UGBAttributeComponent` |
| `Combat/` | Damage (one path, one global `OnDamageDealt` event), status effects, elemental build-up, projectile, explosion shockwave | `UGBCombatLibrary`, `UGBStatusEffectComponent`, `UGBElementalStatusComponent`, `FGBBuildUpMeter` (pure, tested), `AGBProjectile`, `AGBExplosionEffect` |
| `Player/` | Character, controller, input | `AGBPlayerCharacter`, `AGBPlayerController`, `UGBInputSet` |
| `Weapons/` | Held items and slots | `UGBEquipmentComponent`, `AGBHeldItem`, `AGBStaff`, `AGBGun`, `AGBSword` |
| `Spells/` | Spellbook, cast resolution, behaviors | `UGBSpellbookComponent`, `FGBSpellCast`, `AGBSpellActor`, `AGBSpell_*` |
| `QTE/` | Quick-time event | `FGBQTESession`, `FGBGestureRecognizer`, `UGBQTEComponent` |
| `World/` | Eras, progress, interactables, test arena | `UGBEraSubsystem`, `UGBEraComponent`, `UGBProgressSubsystem`, `IGBInteractable`, `AGBPortal`, `AGBChest`, `AGBPickup`, `AGBCheckpointVolume`, `AGBTestArena` |
| `AI/` | Enemies (navmesh path, local steering fallback, stuck check) and encounters | `AGBEnemyCharacter`, `GBSteering` (pure decision, tested), `AGBEncounter` |
| `Items/` | Inventory, consumables, armor, recipes | `UGBInventoryComponent` |
| `Progression/` | Skill trees | `UGBSkillTreeComponent` |
| `UI/` | Canvas HUD (two skins), menus, pixel sprites | `AGBHUD`, `UGBPixelSpriteLibrary` |
| `Tests/` | Pure-logic automation tests | `Glyphbound.<Area>.<Name>` |

## Data files (`Glyphbound/Content/Data`)
`Player.json`, `QTE.json`, `Game.json`, `Spells.json`, `Weapons.json`, `StatusEffects.json`, `Items.json` (items + recipes), `Enemies.json`, `Encounters.json`, `Skills.json`, `Sprites.json`. Schemas are the structs in `Data/GBDataTypes.h` (field name = JSON key). Stat and parameter names are the constants in `Core/GBStatNames.h`.

Modifier keys: an actor stat (`MoveSpeed`), `Weapon.<Id>.<Stat>`, `Weapon.<Slot>.<Stat>` (`Weapon.Staff`, `Weapon.Gun`, `Weapon.Sword`), `Weapon.All.<Stat>`, `Spell.<Id>.<Param>`, `Spell.All.<Param>`, `QTE.TimeDilation`...

Standard status effect ids (defined in `StatusEffects.json`, referenced from other files): `Slow`, `Ignite`, `HealthPotion`, `ManaPotion`, `HealthInjection`, `Enchant_Fire`, `Enchant_Lightning`, `Enchant_Ice`. (`Burn`, `Shock`, `Chill` were removed in GDD v0.22.)

Modifier ops: `Add`, `Multiply`, `Override`, and `MultiplyMin` (only the lowest one on a stat counts: slows never stack, the strongest wins).

Elemental keys (GDD 6 "Elemental build-up"): per enemy in `Enemies.json` `Stats` (`<E>DamageTaken`, `<E>BuildUpTaken`, `<E>BuildUpThreshold`, `<E>BuildUpDecayDelay`, `<E>BuildUpDecayRate`, `<E>StatusDuration`, `<E>StatusImmunity`, `IgniteDamageMult`, `ShockDischargeDamage`, `FrostSlow`; E = Fire, Lightning, Ice) and `Strings` (`<E>StatusReaction`); missing keys fall back to `Game.json` `ElementalDefault.<Key>`. Sources: spell param `BuildUp`, weapon stats `FireBuildUp` / `LightningBuildUp` / `IceBuildUp` and `WeakPointBuildUpMult`, item stat `BuildUp`.

## How the main flows run
- **Cast:** input -> `UGBSpellbookComponent::TryCastSlot` (checks staff in hand, known, cooldown, mana; spends mana) -> `UGBQTEComponent::StartQTE` (slow time, sequence = power, gesture = reach; or instant when off) -> `GBSpell::ResolveCast` (data -> QTE scaling -> modifiers) -> spawn the behavior actor -> it deals damage through `UGBCombatLibrary`.
- **Shoot:** input -> `AGBGun` (fire interval, magazine, spread, recoil, pellets; every number via `GetStat`) -> hitscan -> headshot and distance falloff math in `UGBCombatLibrary` -> `DealDamage`.
- **Era travel:** `AGBPortal::Interact` -> `UGBEraSubsystem::RequestTravel` (fade out, swap era for `UGBEraComponent` actors and `_Past` / `_Future` sub-levels, teleport, restore health/mana/ammo, save checkpoint, fade in).
- **Progress:** encounters set flags in `UGBProgressSubsystem` when cleared; `UGBEraComponent` reads flags, which is how era-gated zones and past-to-future unlocks are built. Death restores the last checkpoint snapshot; flags are never rolled back (enemies do not respawn).
- **Elemental build-up:** a hit carries `FGBDamageInfo::BuildUp` (spell param, weapon stat by element, item stat) and a `BuildUpEventId` (one per shot, cast, blast or tick). `DealDamage` passes it to the target's `UGBElementalStatusComponent` (`ApplyBuildUp`; the ice floor, which deals no damage, calls it directly), which dedupes by event id, applies the enemy's resistance and fills one of three `FGBBuildUpMeter`s. A full bar starts the status: Ignite is the `Ignite` status effect (DOT through `DealDamage`); Shock deals its discharge; Frost slow is a `MultiplyMin` modifier; stun, interrupt and freeze are done by `AGBEnemyCharacter` from the component's `OnStatusStarted` event. The component only ticks (every `ElementalTickInterval`) while a bar is not empty. Looks bind to its events; `gb.ShowElemental 1` is a debug view and `gb.BuildUp` a test command.
- **Upgrades:** `UGBSkillTreeComponent::Unlock` adds the node's modifiers to the player's stats; weapons and spells pick them up on their next read.

## Conventions
- Game text in English. Code comments in English. ASCII only in source files.
- Native multicast delegates (C++ project; no Blueprint graphs needed).
- No custom collision channels: projectiles and queries use the default channels and filter by team in code.
- Pointers to definitions are not kept across frames (data can be reloaded): keep the id, look it up.
- Tests: only pure logic that is easy to break and hard to see by playing; each one must be able to fail; names `Glyphbound.<Area>.<Name>` where a name is never also a prefix of another.
- Build: `powershell -File Tools/Jam/Build-Jam.ps1` (Editor closed). One build at a time per machine.
- Art rule in code: characters, items and effects are camera-facing pixel sprites; **structures are 3D shapes with pixel tiles** (`GBPlaceholder::SetupTexturedShape`, tiles are sprites of `Sprites.json` named `tile_*`). The material behind it (`M_PixelSurface`) is the only binary asset and is generated by `Tools/Jam/Make-Materials.ps1`, never edited by hand.
- A structure that looks different in each era has ONE hidden collision body present in both eras and two non-colliding looks (PastOnly / FutureOnly), so footing and navigation never change on era travel.
- UI that reacts to combat listens to `UGBCombatLibrary::OnDamageDealt()`; nothing in combat code knows about the HUD.

// Pure elemental build-up logic (GDD 6): one bar per element, no UObjects, unit-tested.
#pragma once

#include "CoreMinimal.h"
#include "GBBuildUpMeter.generated.h"

UENUM(BlueprintType)
enum class EGBBuildUpPhase : uint8
{
	Building,
	Active,
	Immune
};

/** Bit mask returned by FGBBuildUpMeter::Advance. */
enum class EGBBuildUpEvent : uint8
{
	ValueChanged = 1 << 0,
	StatusEnded = 1 << 1,
	ImmunityEnded = 1 << 2
};

USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
struct GLYPHBOUND_API FGBBuildUpConfig
{
	GENERATED_BODY()

	/** <= 0: this bar never fills (element disabled for this enemy). */
	UPROPERTY() float Threshold = 0.f;
	/** Multiplier on incoming build-up (resistance < 1, vulnerability > 1, 0 = immune). */
	UPROPERTY() float BuildUpTaken = 1.f;
	/** Seconds without build-up before the bar drains. */
	UPROPERTY() float DecayDelay = 0.f;
	/** Points per second. */
	UPROPERTY() float DecayRate = 0.f;
	UPROPERTY() float StatusDuration = 0.f;
	UPROPERTY() float ImmunityDuration = 0.f;
};

USTRUCT(BlueprintType)
struct GLYPHBOUND_API FGBBuildUpMeter
{
	GENERATED_BODY()

	UPROPERTY() float Value = 0.f;
	UPROPERTY() EGBBuildUpPhase Phase = EGBBuildUpPhase::Building;
	UPROPERTY() float PhaseTimeRemaining = 0.f;
	UPROPERTY() float TimeSinceBuildUp = 0.f;

	/**
	 * Adds Amount * BuildUpTaken while Building. Returns true when this add filled the bar:
	 * Value goes to 0 and the phase to Active for StatusDuration. Ignored (false) when not Building,
	 * Amount <= 0, BuildUpTaken <= 0 or Threshold <= 0. Resets the decay delay.
	 * Full means Value >= Threshold - 1e-4 (float tolerance, so exactly the threshold counts).
	 */
	bool Add(float Amount, const FGBBuildUpConfig& Config);

	/** Advances time; returns a bit mask of EGBBuildUpEvent (ValueChanged, StatusEnded, ImmunityEnded). */
	uint8 Advance(float DeltaTime, const FGBBuildUpConfig& Config);

	/** Building with an empty bar: nothing to tick. */
	bool IsIdle() const;
};

/**
 * Remembers the last 8 build-up events of one target, so one event (a shotgun shot) counts once:
 * the biggest amount of that event wins.
 */
struct GLYPHBOUND_API FGBBuildUpEventLog
{

	/**
	 * Returns how much of Amount to really add: all of it for a new id (or id 0);
	 * for a known id only the part above what that id already added (0 if smaller).
	 */
	float Accept(int32 EventId, float Amount);

private:
	struct FEntry
	{
		int32 Id = 0;
		float Amount = 0.f;
	};
	FEntry Entries[8] = {};
	int32 NextSlot = 0;
};
#include "Combat/GBBuildUpMeter.h"

namespace
{
	constexpr float BuildUpFullTolerance = 1.e-4f;
}

bool FGBBuildUpMeter::Add(float Amount, const FGBBuildUpConfig& Config)
{
	if (Phase != EGBBuildUpPhase::Building)
	{
		return false;
	}
	if (Amount <= 0.f || Config.BuildUpTaken <= 0.f || Config.Threshold <= 0.f)
	{
		return false;
	}
	Value += Amount * Config.BuildUpTaken;
	TimeSinceBuildUp = 0.f;
	if (Value >= Config.Threshold - BuildUpFullTolerance)
	{
		Value = 0.f;
		Phase = EGBBuildUpPhase::Active;
		PhaseTimeRemaining = Config.StatusDuration;
		return true;
	}
	return false;
}

uint8 FGBBuildUpMeter::Advance(float DeltaTime, const FGBBuildUpConfig& Config)
{
	uint8 Events = 0;
	if (DeltaTime <= 0.f)
	{
		return Events;
	}
	if (Phase == EGBBuildUpPhase::Building)
	{
		const float Before = TimeSinceBuildUp;
		TimeSinceBuildUp += DeltaTime;
		if (Value > 0.f)
		{
			const float DecayStart = FMath::Max(Before, Config.DecayDelay);
			const float DecayTime = FMath::Max(0.f, TimeSinceBuildUp - DecayStart);
			if (DecayTime > 0.f && Config.DecayRate > 0.f)
			{
				const float Before21 = Value;
				Value = FMath::Max(0.f, Value - Config.DecayRate * DecayTime);
				if (Value != Before21)
				{
					Events |= static_cast<uint8>(EGBBuildUpEvent::ValueChanged);
				}
			}
		}
	}
	else if (Phase == EGBBuildUpPhase::Active)
	{
		PhaseTimeRemaining -= DeltaTime;
		if (PhaseTimeRemaining <= BuildUpFullTolerance)
		{
			Events |= static_cast<uint8>(EGBBuildUpEvent::StatusEnded);
			if (Config.ImmunityDuration > 0.f)
			{
				Phase = EGBBuildUpPhase::Immune;
				PhaseTimeRemaining = Config.ImmunityDuration + PhaseTimeRemaining;
			}
			else
			{
				Phase = EGBBuildUpPhase::Building;
				PhaseTimeRemaining = 0.f;
			}
		}
	}
	else
	{
		PhaseTimeRemaining -= DeltaTime;
		if (PhaseTimeRemaining <= BuildUpFullTolerance)
		{
			Phase = EGBBuildUpPhase::Building;
			PhaseTimeRemaining = 0.f;
			Events |= static_cast<uint8>(EGBBuildUpEvent::ImmunityEnded);
		}
	}
	return Events;
}

bool FGBBuildUpMeter::IsIdle() const
{
	return Phase == EGBBuildUpPhase::Building && Value <= 0.f;
}

float FGBBuildUpEventLog::Accept(int32 EventId, float Amount)
{
	if (EventId == 0)
	{
		return Amount;
	}
	for (FEntry& Entry : Entries)
	{
		if (Entry.Id == EventId)
		{
			if (Amount > Entry.Amount)
			{
				const float Diff = Amount - Entry.Amount;
				Entry.Amount = Amount;
				return Diff;
			}
			return 0.f;
		}
	}
	// New id: remember the biggest amount of this event; overwrite the oldest slot.
	Entries[NextSlot].Id = EventId;
	Entries[NextSlot].Amount = Amount;
	NextSlot = (NextSlot + 1) % 8;
	return Amount;
}

 succeeded in 1278ms:
# Owner's spec: elemental build-up and status effects (2026-11-12)

> Verbatim from the owner (Spanish), pasted into the architect chat. English summary: GDD v0.22, section 6 "Elemental build-up".
> The architect read the code first, listed the conflicts with the existing systems, and asked two questions; the owner's answers:
> 1. The existing small effects on every elemental hit (fire burn, the lightning 40% slow already named "Shock", the ice enchant's chill): **remove them all**. The bar's status effect is the only elemental effect; the ice floor keeps its zone slow.
> 2. The elemental sword and the ember staff: **both build up** their element.

---

ImplementaciÃ³n del sistema de efectos de estado elementales

Necesitamos incorporar un sistema de acumulaciÃ³n elemental (Elemental Build-Up) y efectos de estado para Fuego, Rayo y Hielo al juego existente.

IMPORTANTE: El proyecto ya cuenta con hechizos, armas, encantamientos elementales, enemigos, daÃ±o, cooldowns, manÃ¡, QTE y perks funcionando. Antes de implementar, revisar la arquitectura actual y reutilizar los sistemas existentes. No reconstruir ni reemplazar mecÃ¡nicas que ya funcionan correctamente.

El objetivo es implementar primero el funcionamiento de los efectos de estado y dejar los valores numÃ©ricos configurables para balancearlos posteriormente.

## 1. Sistema general de acumulaciÃ³n elemental
Cada enemigo debe gestionar independientemente tres acumuladores: `FireBuildUp`, `LightningBuildUp`, `IceBuildUp`.
Cada acumulador aumenta cuando el enemigo recibe un impacto vÃ¡lido de su elemento correspondiente. Cuando una acumulaciÃ³n alcanza el umbral configurado, se activa su efecto de estado.
Reglas generales:
- Cada elemento posee su propia acumulaciÃ³n, umbral, duraciÃ³n, inmunidad temporal y velocidad de decaimiento.
- Si transcurre un perÃ­odo configurable sin recibir acumulaciÃ³n de un elemento, su barra comienza a disminuir progresivamente.
- Al activarse un efecto, la acumulaciÃ³n correspondiente vuelve a cero.
- Mientras el efecto estÃ© activo, no se acumula nuevamente ese mismo elemento.
- DespuÃ©s de finalizar, puede existir un perÃ­odo configurable de inmunidad temporal antes de permitir una nueva acumulaciÃ³n.
- Los tres elementos deben funcionar independientemente.
- Los efectos de daÃ±o y control deben ser independientes de la velocidad de fotogramas.
- Evitar aplicaciones duplicadas de acumulaciÃ³n por un Ãºnico impacto o evento de daÃ±o.

No es necesario implementar todavÃ­a reacciones especiales entre diferentes elementos.

## 2. ConfiguraciÃ³n individual por enemigo
No utilizar multiplicadores globales de resistencia por categorÃ­a de enemigo, como un multiplicador universal para Ã©lites o bosses.
Cada enemigo debe poder definir individualmente:
- Resistencia o vulnerabilidad al daÃ±o de Fuego, Rayo y Hielo.
- Resistencia o vulnerabilidad a la acumulaciÃ³n de cada elemento.
- Umbral de acumulaciÃ³n elemental.
- DuraciÃ³n efectiva de cada estado.
- Inmunidad temporal despuÃ©s de un estado.
- Si es inmune a un estado especÃ­fico.
- QuÃ© reacciÃ³n realiza cuando se activa cada efecto.

Por ejemplo, una valquiria podrÃ­a resistir mucho el fuego y ser vulnerable al rayo, mientras que otro boss podrÃ­a presentar exactamente lo contrario.
Igualmente, un enemigo puede recibir daÃ±o elÃ©ctrico sin ser susceptible a una interrupciÃ³n completa.
Mantener independientes las resistencias al daÃ±o y las resistencias a la acumulaciÃ³n, para poder balancearlas por separado.
Utilizar valores por defecto Ãºnicamente como respaldo para enemigos sin configuraciÃ³n especÃ­fica, no como sustituto de sus caracterÃ­sticas individuales.

## 3. Estado de Fuego â€” Ignite
Identidad: daÃ±o sostenido.
Al alcanzar el umbral de acumulaciÃ³n de fuego, el enemigo entra en estado `Ignite`.
Comportamiento:
- Aplica daÃ±o periÃ³dico durante una duraciÃ³n configurable.
- El daÃ±o se calcula mediante intervalos de tiempo definidos, no por frame.
- No debe activar nuevos estados Ignite mientras el actual siga activo.
- Al finalizar, se aplica la inmunidad temporal configurada para ese enemigo.
- Cada enemigo puede tener diferentes resistencias, duraciÃ³n y daÃ±o de quemadura.

Fuentes de acumulaciÃ³n: Fireball, Fire Wall, Flamethrower, armas encantadas con Fuego.
Cada fuente debe tener su propio valor de acumulaciÃ³n.
Por ejemplo, Flamethrower deberÃ­a poder acumular mÃ¡s fuego mediante exposiciÃ³n continua que un Ãºnico impacto de Fireball, pero los valores exactos se determinarÃ¡n posteriormente.
Compatibilidad: revisar el efecto de quemadura que ya posee Fireball y reutilizarlo o integrarlo con Ignite para evitar dos sistemas de DOT que se dupliquen accidentalmente. No cambiar silenciosamente su comportamiento actual; documentar cualquier ajuste necesario.

## 4. Estado de Rayo â€” Shock
Identidad: interrupciÃ³n y control inmediato.
Al alcanzar el umbral de acumulaciÃ³n elÃ©ctrica, el enemigo entra en estado `Shock`.
Comportamiento:
- Produce una descarga elÃ©ctrica.
- Puede interrumpir el ataque o habilidad que el enemigo estÃ© ejecutando.
- Puede provocar una breve reacciÃ³n de aturdimiento o desestabilizaciÃ³n.
- La reacciÃ³n debe ser configurable individualmente por enemigo.
- Determinados enemigos pueden resistir el aturdimiento, pero recibir una interrupciÃ³n menor.
- Otros pueden ser completamente inmunes al efecto de control.
- Al finalizar, debe respetarse la inmunidad temporal configurada para evitar interrupciones continuas.

No establecer reglas universales que obliguen a todos los bosses a reaccionar de la misma manera.
Fuentes de acumulaciÃ³n: Lightning Bolt, Lightning Rune, Static Orb, armas encantadas con Rayo.
Cada fuente posee su propia acumulaciÃ³n configurable.
En Lightning Rune, el radio de detecciÃ³n que activa la trampa y el radio de su explosiÃ³n son diferentes. La acumulaciÃ³n se aplica a los enemigos alcanzados por la explosiÃ³n, respetando el comportamiento actual de `Expanded Sigil`.

## 5. Estado de Hielo â€” Frost
Identidad: ralentizaciÃ³n y control prolongado.
Al alcanzar el umbral de acumulaciÃ³n de hielo, el enemigo entra en estado `Frost`.
Comportamiento:
- Puede congelar temporalmente al enemigo o reducir considerablemente su velocidad.
- La reacciÃ³n se configura individualmente por enemigo.
- Algunos enemigos podrÃ¡n quedar inmovilizados.
- Otros sufrirÃ¡n una ralentizaciÃ³n fuerte.
- Determinados enemigos podrÃ¡n resistir el congelamiento o ser inmunes.
- Al finalizar, se respeta la inmunidad temporal correspondiente.

Fuentes de acumulaciÃ³n: Ice Floor, armas encantadas con Hielo.
Por ahora, Ice Floor serÃ¡ el Ãºnico hechizo que aplique acumulaciÃ³n de hielo.
Ice Wall e Ice Shield no deben aplicar acumulaciÃ³n elemental.
La acumulaciÃ³n generada por Ice Floor debe ser relativamente baja y configurable.
La intenciÃ³n es que Ice Floor pueda acumular hielo gradualmente mientras los enemigos permanecen dentro de su zona, pero que el jugador pueda completar esa acumulaciÃ³n mÃ¡s rÃ¡pidamente utilizando un arma encantada con Hielo.
Compatibilidad con Ice Floor:
- La ralentizaciÃ³n base actual es del 50%.
- `Deep Freeze` la aumenta a 55%, 60% y 65% segÃºn su nivel.
- El lÃ­mite mÃ¡ximo configurado es del 80%.
- Ya existe interacciÃ³n con el QTE.

No reemplazar ni modificar esos comportamientos.
La ralentizaciÃ³n adicional de Frost no debe sumarse de manera descontrolada a la ralentizaciÃ³n de Ice Floor. Utilizar una regla consistente, como aplicar el efecto de ralentizaciÃ³n mÃ¡s fuerte, respetando los lÃ­mites correspondientes.

## 6. AcumulaciÃ³n especÃ­fica de cada hechizo
No establecer un valor fijo de acumulaciÃ³n para todos los hechizos de un mismo elemento.
Cada hechizo debe poder configurar individualmente:
- Elemento aplicado.
- AcumulaciÃ³n por impacto o tick vÃ¡lido.
- Intervalo entre aplicaciones de acumulaciÃ³n, cuando corresponda.
- Si puede aplicar acumulaciÃ³n o no.

Los hechizos continuos deben utilizar intervalos controlados para impedir que la acumulaciÃ³n dependa de los FPS.
En particular, Flamethrower ya utiliza intervalos de daÃ±o de 0,3 segundos. Procurar reutilizar esos eventos para aplicar acumulaciÃ³n, evitando introducir procesos de daÃ±o adicionales innecesarios.
Mantener intactas sus demÃ¡s mecÃ¡nicas existentes, incluidos el consumo inicial de manÃ¡, el consumo por segundo, el QTE, la canalizaciÃ³n mientras se mantiene la tecla y la interrupciÃ³n por daÃ±o fuerte.

## 7. AcumulaciÃ³n mediante Weapon Enchant
Las armas encantadas deben aplicar acumulaciÃ³n del elemento seleccionado.
Elementos disponibles: Fire, Lightning, Ice.
Reglas:
- Las armas sin encantamiento no aplican acumulaciÃ³n elemental, salvo que alguna tenga esa propiedad explÃ­citamente configurada.
- Cada impacto vÃ¡lido de un arma encantada puede contribuir a la acumulaciÃ³n.
- El valor de acumulaciÃ³n debe poder configurarse por arma y elemento.
- La acumulaciÃ³n debe respetar las resistencias del enemigo.
- Los distintos tipos de armas pueden tener valores diferentes.
- Evitar acumulaciÃ³n excesiva causada por mÃºltiples impactos, especialmente en armas como la escopeta.

No modificar el daÃ±o o funcionamiento de las armas si no es necesario para integrar este sistema.

## 8. BonificaciÃ³n por Headshots y puntos dÃ©biles
Los impactos realizados con armas encantadas sobre la cabeza o sobre un punto dÃ©bil del enemigo deben conceder una bonificaciÃ³n configurable de acumulaciÃ³n elemental.
Reglas:
- Un headshot vÃ¡lido obtiene la bonificaciÃ³n.
- Un impacto sobre un punto dÃ©bil definido por el enemigo obtiene la misma bonificaciÃ³n.
- Los puntos dÃ©biles no tienen que ser necesariamente la cabeza.
- Si la cabeza tambiÃ©n estÃ¡ marcada como punto dÃ©bil, la bonificaciÃ³n no debe aplicarse dos veces.
- El modificador debe poder configurarse y balancearse posteriormente.
- La bonificaciÃ³n afecta a la acumulaciÃ³n elemental; no modificar automÃ¡ticamente el multiplicador de daÃ±o crÃ­tico o de headshot existente.

Utilizar el sistema actual de hitboxes o detecciÃ³n de puntos dÃ©biles si ya existe. No crear uno paralelo innecesariamente.

## 9. ConfiguraciÃ³n mediante datos
Mantener la implementaciÃ³n compatible con la arquitectura actual del proyecto.
Aprovechar `Spells.json`, `Game.json` y las configuraciones existentes de enemigos y armas, cuando corresponda.
Los parÃ¡metros deberÃ­an ser editables sin modificar la lÃ³gica del sistema.
Incluir, segÃºn corresponda: `BuildUpAmount`, `BuildUpThreshold`, `BuildUpDecayRate`, `DecayDelay`, `StatusDuration`, `StatusImmunityDuration`, `StatusEffectType`, `StatusDamage`, `StatusTickInterval`, `StatusSlowAmount`, `StatusControlReaction`, `ElementalResistance`, `HeadshotBuildUpBonus`, `WeakPointBuildUpBonus`.
Los nombres son orientativos. Adaptarlos a las convenciones existentes del proyecto.
Si un parÃ¡metro ya existe con otro nombre, reutilizarlo.
Los valores iniciales pueden ser provisionales para comprobar que los tres estados funcionan, pero deben quedar claramente identificados como pendientes de balanceo.

## 10. Efectos visuales
Por ahora, implementar Ãºnicamente los eventos y estados necesarios para que el sistema pueda comunicarse con los efectos visuales posteriores.
La representaciÃ³n visual se decidirÃ¡ mÃ¡s adelante y podrÃ¡ utilizar Niagara, shaders o efectos particulares por enemigo.

Cada enemigo deberÃ¡ poder responder visualmente a: acumulaciÃ³n elemental, activaciÃ³n del estado, estado activo, finalizaciÃ³n del estado.
La ausencia de efectos visuales definitivos no debe impedir que funcione el sistema.

## 11. Compatibilidad y rendimiento
Este sistema debe funcionar correctamente con mÃºltiples enemigos simultÃ¡neamente, considerando que el juego tendrÃ¡ hordas.
Priorizar:
- ReutilizaciÃ³n de los eventos de daÃ±o existentes.
- Ausencia de comprobaciones costosas por frame cuando puedan utilizarse eventos o intervalos.
- GestiÃ³n independiente de los estados de cada enemigo.
- Evitar aplicaciones duplicadas durante explosiones, proyectiles penetrantes y ataques continuos.

- SeparaciÃ³n entre lÃ³gica de combate y representaciÃ³n visual.

# Task: elemental build-up (Ignite, Shock, Frost) on enemies

Project: **Glyphbound** (Unreal Engine 5.9.0, C++), in `Glyphbound/`. Read `Design/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `Prompts/implementer_rules.md` first (you DO build and run things in this task; the rest of "Hard rules" applies). One build or run at a time. Do not open the Unreal Editor. Do not commit.

Owner's spec, GDD v0.22: read `Design/GDD.md` section 6 "Elemental build-up" and the v0.22 lines of section 18 "Numbers". The owner's original text (Spanish) is `Prompts/feedback8_elemental_spec_owner.md`; this brief is the architect's design for it. **Implement exactly this.** If something here contradicts the code you find, say so in the report and pick the option that keeps existing behavior.

Summary: every enemy gets three independent bars (fire, lightning, ice). Hits with a build-up value fill the bar of their element; a full bar starts a status (Ignite = damage over time, Shock = discharge + stun or interrupt, Frost = freeze or strong slow), then an immunity time. Everything is data. The old small per-hit effects (Burn, the old Shock slow, Chill) are removed.

---

## 1. Shared types and names

### `Core/GBTypes.h`
- `FGBDamageInfo`: add three fields (UPROPERTY like the others, with a one-line comment each):
  - `float BuildUp = 0.f;` raw elemental build-up this hit adds to the target's bar of `Element` (before the target's resistance and the weak point bonus).
  - `float WeakPointBuildUpMult = 1.f;` multiplies `BuildUp` once when `bHeadshot` is true (weapon hits only).
  - `int32 BuildUpEventId = 0;` hits that share an id add build-up to one target only once (shotgun pellets of one shot); 0 = no dedupe.
- `EGBModOp`: add `MultiplyMin` at the end. Meaning: like `Multiply`, but among all `MultiplyMin` modifiers on one stat **only the lowest counts** (the strongest slow wins); that one multiplies with the plain `Multiply` ones. Update the comment at the top of `Stats/GBStatsComponent.h`.

### `Stats/GBStatsComponent.cpp`
- `AddModifiers`: `MultiplyMin` is scaled around 1 exactly like `Multiply`.
- `ApplyModifiers`: keep the lowest `MultiplyMin` value (none if there is no such modifier) and multiply it into the product. Order and Override behavior unchanged.

### `Core/GBStatNames.h`
- `GBStat::BuildUp` ("BuildUp"): spell param and item stat, raw build-up per hit or per tick.
- `GBStat::WeakPointBuildUpMult` ("WeakPointBuildUpMult"): weapon stat; when a weapon does not have it, `Game.json` `WeakPointBuildUpMult` is the default.
- A small namespace `GBElemental` with:
  - `int32 ElementIndex(EGBElement Element)`: Fire 0, Lightning 1, Ice 2, anything else -1. `constexpr int32 NumElements = 3;`.
  - `EGBElement IndexToElement(int32 Index)`.
  - Per-element key tables (built once, `static const FName[3]` or a function returning a const FName from a static array; never `FName(*FString::Printf(...))` per call):
    `DamageTaken(i)` -> `FireDamageTaken`, `LightningDamageTaken`, `IceDamageTaken`;
    `BuildUpTaken(i)` -> `FireBuildUpTaken`...; `Threshold(i)` -> `FireBuildUpThreshold`...; `DecayDelay(i)` -> `FireBuildUpDecayDelay`...; `DecayRate(i)` -> `FireBuildUpDecayRate`...; `StatusDuration(i)` -> `FireStatusDuration`...; `StatusImmunity(i)` -> `FireStatusImmunity`...; `Reaction(i)` -> `FireStatusReaction`, `LightningStatusReaction`, `IceStatusReaction` (enemy `Strings`); `WeaponBuildUp(i)` -> `FireBuildUp`, `LightningBuildUp`, `IceBuildUp` (weapon stats).
  - Single keys: `IgniteDamageMult`, `ShockDischargeDamage`, `FrostSlow`.
  - Default prefix: the default of enemy key `K` is `Game.json` `"ElementalDefault.K"` (Values for numbers, Strings for reactions). Provide `FName DefaultKey(FName Key)` built once per key (cache in a small static TMap or per-table static arrays).
  - Reaction names: `Ignite`, `Stun`, `Interrupt`, `Freeze`, `Slow`, `None`; status effect id `Ignite`.

---

## 2. Pure logic: `Combat/GBBuildUpMeter.h` / `.cpp` (new files, no UObjects)

```cpp
enum class EGBBuildUpPhase : uint8 { Building, Active, Immune };

struct FGBBuildUpConfig
{
	float Threshold = 0.f;        // <= 0: this bar never fills (element disabled for this enemy)
	float BuildUpTaken = 1.f;     // multiplier on incoming build-up (resistance < 1, vulnerability > 1, 0 = immune)
	float DecayDelay = 0.f;       // seconds without build-up before the bar drains
	float DecayRate = 0.f;        // points per second
	float StatusDuration = 0.f;
	float ImmunityDuration = 0.f;
};

struct FGBBuildUpMeter
{
	float Value = 0.f;
	EGBBuildUpPhase Phase = EGBBuildUpPhase::Building;
	float PhaseTimeRemaining = 0.f;
	float TimeSinceBuildUp = 0.f;

	/** Adds Amount * BuildUpTaken while Building. Returns true when this add filled the bar: Value goes to 0 and the phase to Active for StatusDuration. Ignored (false) when not Building, Amount <= 0, BuildUpTaken <= 0 or Threshold <= 0. Resets the decay delay. Full means Value >= Threshold - 1e-4 (float tolerance, so exactly the threshold counts). */
	bool Add(float Amount, const FGBBuildUpConfig& Config);

	/** Advances time; returns a bit mask of EGBBuildUpEvent (ValueChanged, StatusEnded, ImmunityEnded). */
	uint8 Advance(float DeltaTime, const FGBBuildUpConfig& Config);

	/** Building with an empty bar: nothing to tick. */
	bool IsIdle() const;
};
```
`Advance` rules (frame-rate independent: one step of 1.0 s must give the same result as ten steps of 0.1 s):
- **Building:** `Before = TimeSinceBuildUp; TimeSinceBuildUp += Dt;` if `Value > 0`: decay time this step = `max(0, TimeSinceBuildUp - max(Before, DecayDelay))`; `Value = max(0, Value - DecayRate * DecayTime)`; ValueChanged if it changed.
- **Active:** `PhaseTimeRemaining -= Dt`; when `<= 1e-4`: StatusEnded; if `ImmunityDuration > 0` -> Immune with `PhaseTimeRemaining = ImmunityDuration + PhaseTimeRemaining` (carry the overshoot) else Building. A status with `StatusDuration <= 0` ends on the next Advance.
- **Immune:** `PhaseTimeRemaining -= Dt`; when `<= 1e-4` -> Building, ImmunityEnded.

```cpp
/** Remembers the last 8 build-up events of one target, so one event (a shotgun shot) counts once: the biggest amount of that event wins. */
struct FGBBuildUpEventLog
{
	/** Returns how much of Amount to really add: all of it for a new id (or id 0); for a known id only the part above what that id already added (0 if smaller). */
	float Accept(int32 EventId, float Amount);
	// ring of 8 {Id, Amount}; a new id overwrites the oldest entry
};
```

## 3. Component: `Combat/GBElementalStatusComponent.h` / `.cpp` (new)

`UGBElementalStatusComponent : UActorComponent` (ClassGroup Glyphbound, BlueprintSpawnableComponent). Combat logic only, no visuals. Public API:
```cpp
static UGBElementalStatusComponent* Find(const AActor* Actor);
/** Raw build-up of Element (Fire / Lightning / Ice; others ignored), before the owner's BuildUpTaken. EventId != 0 is deduped through FGBBuildUpEventLog. Returns true if it started the status. */
bool AddBuildUp(EGBElement Element, float Amount, int32 EventId, AActor* Instigator);
float GetBuildUp(EGBElement Element) const;
float GetThreshold(EGBElement Element) const;
EGBBuildUpPhase GetPhase(EGBElement Element) const;
float GetPhaseTimeRemaining(EGBElement Element) const;
bool IsStatusActive(EGBElement Element) const;
FName GetReaction(EGBElement Element) const;   // see below
void ClearAll();

// Events for the look of each enemy (Niagara, shaders... later). No visuals here.
FGBOnBuildUpChanged OnBuildUpChanged;          // (EGBElement, float Value, float Threshold)
FGBOnElementalStatusStarted OnStatusStarted;   // (EGBElement, FName Reaction, float Duration)
FGBOnElementalStatusEnded OnStatusEnded;       // (EGBElement)
FGBOnElementalStatusEnded OnImmunityEnded;     // (EGBElement)
```
Declare the delegates (native multicast) in the header.

**Config per element** (read when needed, never cached across a data reload): numbers from the owner's `UGBStatsComponent::GetStat(Key, Default)` where `Default = Data->GetGameValue(GBElemental::DefaultKey(Key), 0)` (so an enemy's `Stats` value wins, and modifiers can change it later); the reaction from the owner's enemy definition `Strings` (`AGBEnemyCharacter::GetDefinition()`), else `Data->GetGameString(DefaultKey, "None")`. Valid reactions: Fire `Ignite` / `None`; Lightning `Stun` / `Interrupt` / `None`; Ice `Freeze` / `Slow` / `None`. An unknown text logs one warning (once per enemy id and element) and counts as `None`. Reaction `None` = immune to that status: no build-up is added (the damage itself is untouched). A missing default (threshold 0) disables that bar; log one warning per key, once.

**Tick:** `PrimaryComponentTick.TickInterval = Game.json ElementalTickInterval` (set in BeginPlay; default 0.1). Tick is enabled only while some meter is not idle (enable in AddBuildUp / StartStatus, disable when all three are idle). Each tick: `Advance` each meter with DeltaTime (the engine passes the real elapsed time), broadcast `OnBuildUpChanged` on ValueChanged, and on StatusEnded / ImmunityEnded run the end logic and broadcast.

**Status start** (when `Add` returns true), with `Duration = <E>StatusDuration`, `Instigator` = the one whose hit filled the bar:
- **Fire, `Ignite`:** `UGBStatusEffectComponent::ApplyEffect("Ignite", Instigator, DurationScale = Duration / IgniteDefinition.Duration (guard against 0), StrengthScale = IgniteDamageMult)`. The Ignite effect is data in `StatusEffects.json` (ticks of fire damage through `DealDamage`, so FireDamageTaken applies and it never adds build-up).
- **Lightning:** first the discharge: if `ShockDischargeDamage > 0`, `UGBCombatLibrary::DealDamage(Owner, Info)` with that amount, Element Lightning, the instigator, `SourceId "Shock"`, `BuildUp 0`. If that killed the owner, stop here. The reaction (`Stun` / `Interrupt`) is done by the enemy through `OnStatusStarted` (below).
- **Ice, `Slow`:** `Stats->AddModifiers("Elemental.Frost", { MoveSpeed, MultiplyMin, 1 - clamp(FrostSlow, 0, 0.95) })`. `Freeze` is done by the enemy through `OnStatusStarted`.
- Then broadcast `OnStatusStarted(Element, Reaction, Duration)` (also for Ignite and Slow) and `OnBuildUpChanged` (value 0).

**Status end:** Fire: `RemoveEffect("Ignite")` (in case a data reload changed durations); Ice: `RemoveModifiers("Elemental.Frost")`; broadcast `OnStatusEnded`.

**Death and cleanup:** bind the owner's attribute `OnDeath` in BeginPlay -> `ClearAll()` (meters reset, Frost modifier removed, tick off). EndPlay: unbind and remove the Frost modifier. No timers survive the owner.

## 4. Damage path: `Combat/GBCombatLibrary.h/.cpp`
- `static int32 NewBuildUpEventId();` global counter, never returns 0.
- `static bool IsWeakPointHit(const AActor* Target, const FVector& HitLocation);` today: `AGBEnemyCharacter::IsHeadshotLocation`. The one place to add other weak points later; a head that is also a weak point is still one hit, so the bonus can never apply twice. Update the header comment.
- `ApplyWeakPointBonus`: `Info.bHeadshot = IsWeakPointHit(...)`; only if `bHeadshot && WeakPointMult > 1` multiply `Amount`. Returns `bHeadshot`. (Today all weapons have mult >= 1.5, so nothing visible changes; it decouples the build-up bonus from the damage bonus.)
- `static float ApplyBuildUp(AActor* Target, const FGBDamageInfo& Info);` returns the raw amount sent. Does nothing unless `Info.BuildUp > 0`, the element is Fire / Lightning / Ice, `CanDamage(Info.Instigator, Target)`, and the target has a `UGBElementalStatusComponent`. Amount = `BuildUp * (bHeadshot ? max(1, WeakPointBuildUpMult) : 1)`; calls `AddBuildUp(Element, Amount, BuildUpEventId, Instigator)`.
- `DealDamage`: right after a hit that dealt damage (`Dealt > 0`, where `OnDamageDealt` is broadcast), call `ApplyBuildUp(Target, FinalInfo)`. Leave the generic `OnHitEffect` code as it is (the ice floor's slow still uses on-hit effects).
- `Stats/GBAttributeComponent.cpp` `ApplyDamage`: multiply by the element's `<E>DamageTaken` stat (default 1) next to `DamageTaken`.

## 5. Sources

### Spells (`Spells/GBSpellTypes.cpp`, `GBSpellActor`, `GBSpell_Zone`)
- `FGBSpellCast::MakeDamageInfo`: `Info.BuildUp = Get(GBStat::BuildUp, 0)` and `Info.BuildUpEventId = UGBCombatLibrary::NewBuildUpEventId()`. Every behavior already builds its hits with it: a projectile gets one id for its whole flight (a lightning bolt passing through three enemies gives one build-up to each, never two to one; check `AGBProjectile::DamagedActors` still prevents a second hit on the same actor), an explosion or rune blast one id (each target in the radius once), and every tick of a wall, orb, flamethrower or zone builds a new info (one build-up per target per tick). Verify these call sites and say in the report if any of them builds the info differently.
- Flamethrower: build-up rides on its existing 0.3 s damage ticks (`AGBSpell_Beam::DamageTick` -> `DamageTarget`). Do not add another timer. Mana, QTE, hold-to-sustain and the strong-hit interrupt must stay exactly as they are.
- Ice floor deals no damage (`DamagePerSecond` 0) so `DealDamage` never runs: in `AGBSpell_Zone::ZoneTick`, the branch that only applies the on-hit effect also calls `UGBCombatLibrary::ApplyBuildUp(Target, Info)` with `Info = SpellCast.MakeDamageInfo(NAME_None, this)` (one info per tick, HitLocation = target location). Its slow, `EffectStrength`, Deep Freeze and the 80% cap are untouched.
- Ice wall and ice shield have no `BuildUp` param: they build up nothing.

### Weapons (`Weapons/GBHeldItem.cpp`, `GBGun.cpp`)
- `AGBHeldItem::MakeDamageInfo`: after the element is final (weapon element, or the gun enchant's element): if `GBElemental::ElementIndex(Info.Element) >= 0`, `Info.BuildUp = GetStat(GBElemental::WeaponBuildUp(i), 0)`; `Info.WeakPointBuildUpMult = GetStat(GBStat::WeakPointBuildUpMult, Data->GetGameValue("WeakPointBuildUpMult", 1))`; `Info.BuildUpEventId = NewBuildUpEventId()`. Use `GetStat` so `Weapon.*` modifiers reach these values. An unenchanted gun is Physical, the apprentice staff Arcane: no build-up. The sword uses its current gem element.
- Check that `MakeDamageInfo` runs once per trigger pull: `AGBGun` builds `BaseInfo` once per shot and passes it to `FireHitscan` / `FireProjectile`, so all pellets share one `BuildUpEventId` and the event log keeps only the best pellet (a head pellet after a body pellet adds only the difference). Same for the sword (one id per swing) and the staff (one per bolt). If any of them calls it per pellet, fix that.
- Weapon damage, falloff, the head damage bonus and everything else stay the same.

### Electric grenade (`Items/GBInventoryComponent.cpp`, throw path around `Config.Damage`)
- `Config.Damage.BuildUp = ReadItemStat(Def, GBStat::BuildUp, 0)` and a new event id. The grenade no longer uses an on-hit effect (data below).

## 6. Enemies (`AI/GBEnemyCharacter.h/.cpp`)
- Create a `UGBElementalStatusComponent` in the constructor (`ElementalStatus`, UPROPERTY VisibleAnywhere, getter `GetElementalStatus()`).
- BeginPlay: bind `ElementalStatus->OnStatusStarted` to `HandleElementalStatusStarted(EGBElement, FName Reaction, float Duration)`: Lightning `Stun` -> `ApplyControlLock(Duration)`; Lightning `Interrupt` -> `InterruptAttack()`; Ice `Freeze` -> `ApplyControlLock(Duration)`. Everything else: nothing here.
- `void InterruptAttack()`: if the state is Windup, `SetState(Recover)` (the telegraph goes off in SetState; the attack is lost and the interval starts again).
- `void ApplyControlLock(float Seconds)`: ignored when dead; `ControlLockTime = max(ControlLockTime, Seconds)`; `InterruptAttack()`; `StopMoving()` once.
- `Tick`: right after the dead / era-presence early return, if `ControlLockTime > 0`: subtract DeltaSeconds, `ResetStuck()`, and return **before** `StateTime` and the other timers advance (state timers pause while locked, so the attack interval runs after the lock). Gravity and knockback still work (character movement).
- `float GetControlLockTime() const` (for the debug view). Death sets it to 0.

## 9. Smoke script (`Core/GBDevCommands.cpp`, near the end, where it does not disturb later steps)
On the training dummy (spawned by the arena; find it by enemy id `TrainingDummy`):
- First, dedupe: on the empty fire bar, two adds of 20 with the SAME event id must leave it at 20 (times the dummy's BuildUpTaken, 1 by default): log `SMOKE elemental dedupe ok=%d`.
- Then for each element: send build-up of 30 with new event ids (through `UGBCombatLibrary::ApplyBuildUp`, player as instigator) until it starts the status (at most 10 adds). Log `SMOKE elemental <E> triggered=%d adds=%d`. For Fire also log `ignite=%d` (`UGBStatusEffectComponent::HasEffect("Ignite")`). Right after, one more add must be refused: log `blockedWhileActive=%d`. Any failure -> `UE_LOG(LogGB, Error, ...)`.
- Leave a second of game time after these steps before the next screenshot.

## 10. Tests (pure logic, new file `Tests/GBElementalTests.cpp`, plus one in `Tests/GBStatsTests.cpp`)
Each test must be able to fail. Check that no test name is a prefix of another.
- `Glyphbound.Elemental.MeterThreshold`: below the threshold no trigger; reaching it exactly triggers, the value is 0, the phase Active; adds during Active and during Immune are ignored; after StatusDuration the phase is Immune, after ImmunityDuration Building again and adds count; ImmunityDuration 0 goes straight back to Building; two configs (threshold 100 vs 160 with BuildUpTaken 0.75) need different numbers of the same add to trigger; BuildUpTaken 0 never fills.
- `Glyphbound.Elemental.MeterDecay`: nothing drains during the delay; after it the bar drains at DecayRate; one Advance of 1.0 s equals ten of 0.1 s (within 1e-3) both for a run that crosses the delay boundary and one fully after it; never below 0; an add resets the delay.
- `Glyphbound.Elemental.EventDedupe`: the same id twice adds once; the same id with a bigger amount adds only the difference; id 0 is never deduped; after 8 other ids the first one is forgotten (adds again).
- `Glyphbound.Stats.StrongestSlow`: with the existing stats API: a `MultiplyMin` 0.5 scaled by strength 1.1 gives 0.45 (Deep Freeze rank 1); add a `MultiplyMin` 0.4 -> the result is 0.4, not 0.18; add a plain `Multiply` 0.8 -> 0.32; remove the 0.4 source -> 0.36.

#include "Combat/GBElementalStatusComponent.h"

#include "AI/GBEnemyCharacter.h"
#include "Combat/GBCombatLibrary.h"
#include "Combat/GBStatusEffectComponent.h"
#include "Core/GBStatNames.h"
#include "Data/GBDataSubsystem.h"
#include "Stats/GBAttributeComponent.h"
#include "Stats/GBStatsComponent.h"

namespace
{
	const FName ElementalTickIntervalKey(TEXT("ElementalTickInterval"));
	constexpr float DefaultTickInterval = 0.1f;
	constexpr float FrostSlowMax = 0.95f;

	bool IsValidReaction(int32 Index, FName Reaction)
	{
		using namespace GBElemental;
		if (Index == 0)
		{
			return Reaction == ReactionIgnite || Reaction == ReactionNone;
		}
		if (Index == 1)
		{
			return Reaction == ReactionStun || Reaction == ReactionInterrupt || Reaction == ReactionNone;
		}
		if (Index == 2)
		{
			return Reaction == ReactionFreeze || Reaction == ReactionSlow || Reaction == ReactionNone;
		}
		return false;
	}
}

UGBElementalStatusComponent::UGBElementalStatusComponent()
{
	PrimaryComponentTick.bCanEverTick = true;
	PrimaryComponentTick.bStartWithTickEnabled = false;
}

UGBElementalStatusComponent* UGBElementalStatusComponent::Find(const AActor* Actor)
{
	return Actor ? Actor->FindComponentByClass<UGBElementalStatusComponent>() : nullptr;
}

void UGBElementalStatusComponent::BeginPlay()
{
	Super::BeginPlay();

	const UGBDataSubsystem* Data = UGBDataSubsystem::Get(this);
	const float Interval = Data ? Data->GetGameValue(ElementalTickIntervalKey, DefaultTickInterval) : DefaultTickInterval;
	PrimaryComponentTick.TickInterval = FMath::Max(0.01f, Interval);
	SetComponentTickEnabled(false);

	if (UGBAttributeComponent* Attributes = UGBAttributeComponent::Find(GetOwner()))
	{
		DeathHandle = Attributes->OnDeath.AddUObject(this, &UGBElementalStatusComponent::HandleOwnerDeath);
	}
}

void UGBElementalStatusComponent::EndPlay(const EEndPlayReason::Type EndPlayReason)
{
	if (UGBAttributeComponent* Attributes = UGBAttributeComponent::Find(GetOwner()))
	{
		Attributes->OnDeath.Remove(DeathHandle);
	}
	DeathHandle.Reset();
	if (UGBStatsComponent* Stats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(GetOwner()))
	{
		Stats->RemoveModifiers(GBElemental::FrostSource);
	}
	Super::EndPlay(EndPlayReason);
}

void UGBElementalStatusComponent::HandleOwnerDeath(const FGBDamageInfo& KillingBlow)
{
	(void)KillingBlow;
	ClearAll();
}

bool UGBElementalStatusComponent::ReadConfig(int32 Index, FGBBuildUpConfig& OutConfig, FName& OutReaction) const
{
	OutConfig = FGBBuildUpConfig();
	OutReaction = GBElemental::ReactionNone;

	if (Index < 0 || Index >= GBElemental::NumElements)
	{
		return false;
	}

	const AActor* Owner = GetOwner();
	const UGBStatsComponent* Stats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(Owner);
	const UGBDataSubsystem* Data = UGBDataSubsystem::Get(this);

	auto ReadNumber = [&](FName Key, float Fallback) -> float
	{
		const float GameDefault = Data ? Data->GetGameValue(GBElemental::DefaultKey(Key), Fallback) : Fallback;
		return Stats ? Stats->GetStat(Key, GameDefault) : GameDefault;
	};

	const FName ThresholdKey = GBElemental::ThresholdKey(Index);
	const FName BuildUpTakenKey = GBElemental::BuildUpTakenKey(Index);
	const FName DecayDelayKey = GBElemental::DecayDelayKey(Index);
	const FName DecayRateKey = GBElemental::DecayRateKey(Index);
	const FName DurationKey = GBElemental::StatusDurationKey(Index);
	const FName ImmunityKey = GBElemental::StatusImmunityKey(Index);

	OutConfig.Threshold = ReadNumber(ThresholdKey, 0.f);
	OutConfig.BuildUpTaken = ReadNumber(BuildUpTakenKey, 0.f);
	OutConfig.DecayDelay = ReadNumber(DecayDelayKey, 0.f);
	OutConfig.DecayRate = ReadNumber(DecayRateKey, 0.f);
	OutConfig.StatusDuration = ReadNumber(DurationKey, 0.f);
	OutConfig.ImmunityDuration = ReadNumber(ImmunityKey, 0.f);

	// Reaction: enemy definition Strings, else Game.json default.
	const FName ReactionKey = GBElemental::ReactionKey(Index);
	FString ReactionStr;
	bool bHasReaction = false;
	FName EnemyId = NAME_None;
	if (const AGBEnemyCharacter* Enemy = Cast<AGBEnemyCharacter>(Owner))
	{
		EnemyId = Enemy->GetEnemyId();
		if (const FGBEnemyDefinition* Def = Enemy->GetDefinition())
		{
			if (const FString* Found = Def->Strings.Find(ReactionKey))
			{
				ReactionStr = *Found;
				bHasReaction = true;
			}
		}
	}
	if (!bHasReaction)
	{
		const FString DefaultStr = Data ? Data->GetGameString(GBElemental::DefaultKey(ReactionKey), FString()) : FString();
		if (!DefaultStr.IsEmpty())
		{
			ReactionStr = DefaultStr;
			bHasReaction = true;
		}
	}

	FName Reaction = bHasReaction ? FName(*ReactionStr) : GBElemental::ReactionNone;
	if (!IsValidReaction(Index, Reaction))
	{
		if (!EnemyId.IsNone())
		{
			const FName WarnKey(*FString::Printf(TEXT("%s:%d"), *EnemyId.ToString(), Index));
			if (!WarnedReactions.Contains(WarnKey))
			{
				WarnedReactions.Add(WarnKey);
				UE_LOG(LogGB, Warning, TEXT("Enemy '%s': unknown %s '%s', treating as None."),
					*EnemyId.ToString(), *ReactionKey.ToString(), *Reaction.ToString());
			}
		}
		else
		{
			UE_LOG(LogGB, Warning, TEXT("Elemental: unknown %s '%s', treating as None."), *ReactionKey.ToString(), *Reaction.ToString());
		}
		Reaction = GBElemental::ReactionNone;
	}
	OutReaction = Reaction;
	return true;
}

bool UGBElementalStatusComponent::AddBuildUp(EGBElement Element, float Amount, int32 EventId, AActor* Instigator)
{
	const int32 Index = GBElemental::ElementIndex(Element);
	if (Index < 0 || Index >= GBElemental::NumElements)
	{
		return false;
	}
	if (Amount <= 0.f)
	{
		return false;
	}
	FGBBuildUpConfig Config;
	FName Reaction;
	if (!ReadConfig(Index, Config, Reaction))
	{
		return false;
	}
	// Immune to the status: no build-up (damage itself is untouched).
	if (Reaction == GBElemental::ReactionNone)
	{
		return false;
	}
	if (Config.Threshold <= 0.f)
	{
		return false;
	}

	float ToAdd = Amount;
	if (EventId != 0)
	{
		ToAdd = EventLog.Accept(EventId, Amount);
		if (ToAdd <= 0.f)
		{
			return false;
		}
	}

	const bool bTriggered = Meters[Index].Add(ToAdd, Config);
	UpdateTickEnabled();
	if (bTriggered)
	{
		StartStatus(Index, Config, Reaction, Config.StatusDuration, Instigator);
		return true;
	}
	OnBuildUpChanged.Broadcast(Element, Meters[Index].Value, Config.Threshold);
	return false;
}

void UGBElementalStatusComponent::StartStatus(int32 Index, const FGBBuildUpConfig& Config, FName Reaction, float Duration, AActor* Instigator)
{
	(void)Config;
	AActor* Owner = GetOwner();
	if (!Owner)
	{
		return;
	}
	const EGBElement Element = GBElemental::IndexToElement(Index);
	UGBStatsComponent* Stats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(Owner);
	const UGBDataSubsystem* Data = UGBDataSubsystem::Get(this);

	if (Index == 0)
	{
		// Fire: Ignite DOT through the status effect system.
		float IgniteDamageMult = 1.f;
		if (Stats)
		{
			const float GameDefault = Data ? Data->GetGameValue(GBElemental::DefaultKey(GBElemental::IgniteDamageMult), 1.f) : 1.f;
			IgniteDamageMult = Stats->GetStat(GBElemental::IgniteDamageMult, GameDefault);
		}
		else if (Data)
		{
			IgniteDamageMult = Data->GetGameValue(GBElemental::DefaultKey(GBElemental::IgniteDamageMult), 1.f);
		}
		float DurationScale = 1.f;
		if (Data)
		{
			if (const FGBStatusEffectDefinition* IgniteDef = Data->FindStatusEffect(GBElemental::IgniteEffect))
			{
				if (IgniteDef->Duration > 1.e-4f)
				{
					DurationScale = Duration / IgniteDef->Duration;
				}
			}
		}
		if (UGBStatusEffectComponent* Effects = UGBStatusEffectComponent::Find(Owner))
		{
			AActor* RealInstigator = IsValid(Instigator) ? Instigator : nullptr;
			Effects->ApplyEffect(GBElemental::IgniteEffect, RealInstigator, DurationScale, FMath::Max(0.f, IgniteDamageMult));
		}
	}
	else if (Index == 1)
	{
		// Lightning: discharge first.
		float Discharge = 0.f;
		if (Stats)
		{
			const float GameDefault = Data ? Data->GetGameValue(GBElemental::DefaultKey(GBElemental::ShockDischargeDamage), 0.f) : 0.f;
			Discharge = Stats->GetStat(GBElemental::ShockDischargeDamage, GameDefault);
		}
		else if (Data)
		{
			Discharge = Data->GetGameValue(GBElemental::DefaultKey(GBElemental::ShockDischargeDamage), 0.f);
		}
		if (Discharge > 0.f)
		{
			FGBDamageInfo Info;
			Info.Amount = Discharge;
			Info.Element = EGBElement::Lightning;
			Info.Instigator = IsValid(Instigator) ? Instigator : nullptr;
			Info.Causer = Owner;
			Info.SourceId = GBElemental::ShockSource;
			Info.HitLocation = Owner->GetActorLocation();
			Info.BuildUp = 0.f;
			UGBCombatLibrary::DealDamage(Owner, Info);
			if (!UGBCombatLibrary::IsAlive(Owner))
			{
				return;
			}
		}
	}
	else if (Index == 2)
	{
		// Ice: Slow is a MultiplyMin modifier; Freeze is done by the enemy.
		if (Reaction == GBElemental::ReactionSlow && Stats)
		{
			float FrostSlow = 0.f;
			const float GameDefault = Data ? Data->GetGameValue(GBElemental::DefaultKey(GBElemental::FrostSlow), 0.f) : 0.f;
			FrostSlow = Stats->GetStat(GBElemental::FrostSlow, GameDefault);
			const float Clamped = FMath::Clamp(FrostSlow, 0.f, FrostSlowMax);
			FGBModifierDef Mod;
			Mod.Stat = GBStat::MoveSpeed;
			Mod.Op = EGBModOp::MultiplyMin;
			Mod.Value = 1.f - Clamped;
			Stats->AddModifiers(GBElemental::FrostSource, MakeArrayView(&Mod, 1));
		}
	}

	UpdateTickEnabled();
	OnStatusStarted.Broadcast(Element, Reaction, Duration);
	OnBuildUpChanged.Broadcast(Element, 0.f, Config.Threshold);
}

void UGBElementalStatusComponent::EndStatus(int32 Index)
{
	const EGBElement Element = GBElemental::IndexToElement(Index);
	if (Index == 0)
	{
		if (UGBStatusEffectComponent* Effects = UGBStatusEffectComponent::Find(GetOwner()))
		{
			Effects->RemoveEffect(GBElemental::IgniteEffect);
		}
	}
	else if (Index == 2)
	{
		if (UGBStatsComponent* Stats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(GetOwner()))
		{
			Stats->RemoveModifiers(GBElemental::FrostSource);
		}
	}
	OnStatusEnded.Broadcast(Element);
}

float UGBElementalStatusComponent::GetBuildUp(EGBElement Element) const
{
	const int32 Index = GBElemental::ElementIndex(Element);
	return (Index >= 0 && Index < GBElemental::NumElements) ? Meters[Index].Value : 0.f;
}

float UGBElementalStatusComponent::GetThreshold(EGBElement Element) const
{
	const int32 Index = GBElemental::ElementIndex(Element);
	if (Index < 0 || Index >= GBElemental::NumElements)
	{
		return 0.f;
	}
	FGBBuildUpConfig Config;
	FName Reaction;
	if (!ReadConfig(Index, Config, Reaction))
	{
		return 0.f;
	}
	return Config.Threshold;
}

EGBBuildUpPhase UGBElementalStatusComponent::GetPhase(EGBElement Element) const
{
	const int32 Index = GBElemental::ElementIndex(Element);
	return (Index >= 0 && Index < GBElemental::NumElements) ? Meters[Index].Phase : EGBBuildUpPhase::Building;
}

float UGBElementalStatusComponent::GetPhaseTimeRemaining(EGBElement Element) const
{
	const int32 Index = GBElemental::ElementIndex(Element);
	return (Index >= 0 && Index < GBElemental::NumElements) ? Meters[Index].PhaseTimeRemaining : 0.f;
}

bool UGBElementalStatusComponent::IsStatusActive(EGBElement Element) const
{
	return GetPhase(Element) == EGBBuildUpPhase::Active;
}

FName UGBElementalStatusComponent::GetReaction(EGBElement Element) const
{
	const int32 Index = GBElemental::ElementIndex(Element);
	if (Index < 0 || Index >= GBElemental::NumElements)
	{
		return GBElemental::ReactionNone;
	}
	FGBBuildUpConfig Config;
	FName Reaction;
	if (!ReadConfig(Index, Config, Reaction))
	{
		return GBElemental::ReactionNone;
	}
	return Reaction;
}

void UGBElementalStatusComponent::ClearAll()
{
	for (int32 i = 0; i < GBElemental::NumElements; ++i)
	{
		Meters[i].Value = 0.f;
		Meters[i].Phase = EGBBuildUpPhase::Building;
		Meters[i].PhaseTimeRemaining = 0.f;
		Meters[i].TimeSinceBuildUp = 0.f;
	}
	EventLog = FGBBuildUpEventLog();
	if (UGBStatsComponent* Stats = UGBStatsComponent::Find(GetOwner()))
	{
		Stats->RemoveModifiers(GBElemental::FrostSource);
	}
	UpdateTickEnabled();
}

void UGBElementalStatusComponent::UpdateTickEnabled()
{
	bool bAnyBusy = false;
	for (int32 i = 0; i < GBElemental::NumElements; ++i)
	{
		if (!Meters[i].IsIdle())
		{
			bAnyBusy = true;
			break;
		}
	}
	SetComponentTickEnabled(bAnyBusy);
}

void UGBElementalStatusComponent::TickComponent(float DeltaTime, ELevelTick TickType, FActorComponentTickFunction* ThisTickFunction)
{
	Super::TickComponent(DeltaTime, TickType, ThisTickFunction);

	if (DeltaTime <= 0.f)
	{
		return;
	}

	for (int32 i = 0; i < GBElemental::NumElements; ++i)
	{
		FGBBuildUpConfig Config;
		FName Reaction;
		if (!ReadConfig(i, Config, Reaction))
		{
			continue;
		}
		const uint8 Events = Meters[i].Advance(DeltaTime, Config);
		if (Events & static_cast<uint8>(EGBBuildUpEvent::ValueChanged))
		{
			OnBuildUpChanged.Broadcast(GBElemental::IndexToElement(i), Meters[i].Value, Config.Threshold);
		}
		if (Events & static_cast<uint8>(EGBBuildUpEvent::StatusEnded))
		{
			EndStatus(i);
		}
		if (Events & static_cast<uint8>(EGBBuildUpEvent::ImmunityEnded))
		{
			OnImmunityEnded.Broadcast(GBElemental::IndexToElement(i));
		}
	}
	UpdateTickEnabled();
}

# Task: elemental build-up (Ignite, Shock, Frost) on enemies

Project: **Glyphbound** (Unreal Engine 5.9.0, C++), in `Glyphbound/`. Read `Design/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `Prompts/implementer_rules.md` first (you DO build and run things in this task; the rest of "Hard rules" applies). One build or run at a time. Do not open the Unreal Editor. Do not commit.

Owner's spec, GDD v0.22: read `Design/GDD.md` section 6 "Elemental build-up" and the v0.22 lines of section 18 "Numbers". The owner's original text (Spanish) is `Prompts/feedback8_elemental_spec_owner.md`; this brief is the architect's design for it. **Implement exactly this.** If something here contradicts the code you find, say so in the report and pick the option that keeps existing behavior.

Summary: every enemy gets three independent bars (fire, lightning, ice). Hits with a build-up value fill the bar of their element; a full bar starts a status (Ignite = damage over time, Shock = discharge + stun or interrupt, Frost = freeze or strong slow), then an immunity time. Everything is data. The old small per-hit effects (Burn, the old Shock slow, Chill) are removed.

---

## 1. Shared types and names

### `Core/GBTypes.h`
- `FGBDamageInfo`: add three fields (UPROPERTY like the others, with a one-line comment each):
  - `float BuildUp = 0.f;` raw elemental build-up this hit adds to the target's bar of `Element` (before the target's resistance and the weak point bonus).
  - `float WeakPointBuildUpMult = 1.f;` multiplies `BuildUp` once when `bHeadshot` is true (weapon hits only).
  - `int32 BuildUpEventId = 0;` hits that share an id add build-up to one target only once (shotgun pellets of one shot); 0 = no dedupe.
- `EGBModOp`: add `MultiplyMin` at the end. Meaning: like `Multiply`, but among all `MultiplyMin` modifiers on one stat **only the lowest counts** (the strongest slow wins); that one multiplies with the plain `Multiply` ones. Update the comment at the top of `Stats/GBStatsComponent.h`.

### `Stats/GBStatsComponent.cpp`
- `AddModifiers`: `MultiplyMin` is scaled around 1 exactly like `Multiply`.
- `ApplyModifiers`: keep the lowest `MultiplyMin` value (none if there is no such modifier) and multiply it into the product. Order and Override behavior unchanged.

### `Core/GBStatNames.h`
- `GBStat::BuildUp` ("BuildUp"): spell param and item stat, raw build-up per hit or per tick.
- `GBStat::WeakPointBuildUpMult` ("WeakPointBuildUpMult"): weapon stat; when a weapon does not have it, `Game.json` `WeakPointBuildUpMult` is the default.
- A small namespace `GBElemental` with:
  - `int32 ElementIndex(EGBElement Element)`: Fire 0, Lightning 1, Ice 2, anything else -1. `constexpr int32 NumElements = 3;`.
  - `EGBElement IndexToElement(int32 Index)`.
  - Per-element key tables (built once, `static const FName[3]` or a function returning a const FName from a static array; never `FName(*FString::Printf(...))` per call):
    `DamageTaken(i)` -> `FireDamageTaken`, `LightningDamageTaken`, `IceDamageTaken`;
    `BuildUpTaken(i)` -> `FireBuildUpTaken`...; `Threshold(i)` -> `FireBuildUpThreshold`...; `DecayDelay(i)` -> `FireBuildUpDecayDelay`...; `DecayRate(i)` -> `FireBuildUpDecayRate`...; `StatusDuration(i)` -> `FireStatusDuration`...; `StatusImmunity(i)` -> `FireStatusImmunity`...; `Reaction(i)` -> `FireStatusReaction`, `LightningStatusReaction`, `IceStatusReaction` (enemy `Strings`); `WeaponBuildUp(i)` -> `FireBuildUp`, `LightningBuildUp`, `IceBuildUp` (weapon stats).
  - Single keys: `IgniteDamageMult`, `ShockDischargeDamage`, `FrostSlow`.
  - Default prefix: the default of enemy key `K` is `Game.json` `"ElementalDefault.K"` (Values for numbers, Strings for reactions). Provide `FName DefaultKey(FName Key)` built once per key (cache in a small static TMap or per-table static arrays).
  - Reaction names: `Ignite`, `Stun`, `Interrupt`, `Freeze`, `Slow`, `None`; status effect id `Ignite`.

---

## 2. Pure logic: `Combat/GBBuildUpMeter.h` / `.cpp` (new files, no UObjects)

```cpp
enum class EGBBuildUpPhase : uint8 { Building, Active, Immune };

struct FGBBuildUpConfig
{
	float Threshold = 0.f;        // <= 0: this bar never fills (element disabled for this enemy)
	float BuildUpTaken = 1.f;     // multiplier on incoming build-up (resistance < 1, vulnerability > 1, 0 = immune)
	float DecayDelay = 0.f;       // seconds without build-up before the bar drains
	float DecayRate = 0.f;        // points per second
	float StatusDuration = 0.f;
	float ImmunityDuration = 0.f;
};

struct FGBBuildUpMeter
{
	float Value = 0.f;
	EGBBuildUpPhase Phase = EGBBuildUpPhase::Building;
	float PhaseTimeRemaining = 0.f;
	float TimeSinceBuildUp = 0.f;

	/** Adds Amount * BuildUpTaken while Building. Returns true when this add filled the bar: Value goes to 0 and the phase to Active for StatusDuration. Ignored (false) when not Building, Amount <= 0, BuildUpTaken <= 0 or Threshold <= 0. Resets the decay delay. Full means Value >= Threshold - 1e-4 (float tolerance, so exactly the threshold counts). */
	bool Add(float Amount, const FGBBuildUpConfig& Config);

	/** Advances time; returns a bit mask of EGBBuildUpEvent (ValueChanged, StatusEnded, ImmunityEnded). */
	uint8 Advance(float DeltaTime, const FGBBuildUpConfig& Config);

	/** Building with an empty bar: nothing to tick. */
	bool IsIdle() const;
};
```
`Advance` rules (frame-rate independent: one step of 1.0 s must give the same result as ten steps of 0.1 s):
- **Building:** `Before = TimeSinceBuildUp; TimeSinceBuildUp += Dt;` if `Value > 0`: decay time this step = `max(0, TimeSinceBuildUp - max(Before, DecayDelay))`; `Value = max(0, Value - DecayRate * DecayTime)`; ValueChanged if it changed.
- **Active:** `PhaseTimeRemaining -= Dt`; when `<= 1e-4`: StatusEnded; if `ImmunityDuration > 0` -> Immune with `PhaseTimeRemaining = ImmunityDuration + PhaseTimeRemaining` (carry the overshoot) else Building. A status with `StatusDuration <= 0` ends on the next Advance.
- **Immune:** `PhaseTimeRemaining -= Dt`; when `<= 1e-4` -> Building, ImmunityEnded.

```cpp
/** Remembers the last 8 build-up events of one target, so one event (a shotgun shot) counts once: the biggest amount of that event wins. */
struct FGBBuildUpEventLog
{
	/** Returns how much of Amount to really add: all of it for a new id (or id 0); for a known id only the part above what that id already added (0 if smaller). */
	float Accept(int32 EventId, float Amount);
	// ring of 8 {Id, Amount}; a new id overwrites the oldest entry
};
```

## 3. Component: `Combat/GBElementalStatusComponent.h` / `.cpp` (new)

`UGBElementalStatusComponent : UActorComponent` (ClassGroup Glyphbound, BlueprintSpawnableComponent). Combat logic only, no visuals. Public API:
```cpp
static UGBElementalStatusComponent* Find(const AActor* Actor);
/** Raw build-up of Element (Fire / Lightning / Ice; others ignored), before the owner's BuildUpTaken. EventId != 0 is deduped through FGBBuildUpEventLog. Returns true if it started the status. */
bool AddBuildUp(EGBElement Element, float Amount, int32 EventId, AActor* Instigator);
float GetBuildUp(EGBElement Element) const;
float GetThreshold(EGBElement Element) const;
EGBBuildUpPhase GetPhase(EGBElement Element) const;
float GetPhaseTimeRemaining(EGBElement Element) const;
bool IsStatusActive(EGBElement Element) const;
FName GetReaction(EGBElement Element) const;   // see below
void ClearAll();

// Events for the look of each enemy (Niagara, shaders... later). No visuals here.
FGBOnBuildUpChanged OnBuildUpChanged;          // (EGBElement, float Value, float Threshold)
FGBOnElementalStatusStarted OnStatusStarted;   // (EGBElement, FName Reaction, float Duration)
FGBOnElementalStatusEnded OnStatusEnded;       // (EGBElement)
FGBOnElementalStatusEnded OnImmunityEnded;     // (EGBElement)
```
Declare the delegates (native multicast) in the header.

**Config per element** (read when needed, never cached across a data reload): numbers from the owner's `UGBStatsComponent::GetStat(Key, Default)` where `Default = Data->GetGameValue(GBElemental::DefaultKey(Key), 0)` (so an enemy's `Stats` value wins, and modifiers can change it later); the reaction from the owner's enemy definition `Strings` (`AGBEnemyCharacter::GetDefinition()`), else `Data->GetGameString(DefaultKey, "None")`. Valid reactions: Fire `Ignite` / `None`; Lightning `Stun` / `Interrupt` / `None`; Ice `Freeze` / `Slow` / `None`. An unknown text logs one warning (once per enemy id and element) and counts as `None`. Reaction `None` = immune to that status: no build-up is added (the damage itself is untouched). A missing default (threshold 0) disables that bar; log one warning per key, once.

**Tick:** `PrimaryComponentTick.TickInterval = Game.json ElementalTickInterval` (set in BeginPlay; default 0.1). Tick is enabled only while some meter is not idle (enable in AddBuildUp / StartStatus, disable when all three are idle). Each tick: `Advance` each meter with DeltaTime (the engine passes the real elapsed time), broadcast `OnBuildUpChanged` on ValueChanged, and on StatusEnded / ImmunityEnded run the end logic and broadcast.

**Status start** (when `Add` returns true), with `Duration = <E>StatusDuration`, `Instigator` = the one whose hit filled the bar:
- **Fire, `Ignite`:** `UGBStatusEffectComponent::ApplyEffect("Ignite", Instigator, DurationScale = Duration / IgniteDefinition.Duration (guard against 0), StrengthScale = IgniteDamageMult)`. The Ignite effect is data in `StatusEffects.json` (ticks of fire damage through `DealDamage`, so FireDamageTaken applies and it never adds build-up).
- **Lightning:** first the discharge: if `ShockDischargeDamage > 0`, `UGBCombatLibrary::DealDamage(Owner, Info)` with that amount, Element Lightning, the instigator, `SourceId "Shock"`, `BuildUp 0`. If that killed the owner, stop here. The reaction (`Stun` / `Interrupt`) is done by the enemy through `OnStatusStarted` (below).
- **Ice, `Slow`:** `Stats->AddModifiers("Elemental.Frost", { MoveSpeed, MultiplyMin, 1 - clamp(FrostSlow, 0, 0.95) })`. `Freeze` is done by the enemy through `OnStatusStarted`.
- Then broadcast `OnStatusStarted(Element, Reaction, Duration)` (also for Ignite and Slow) and `OnBuildUpChanged` (value 0).

**Status end:** Fire: `RemoveEffect("Ignite")` (in case a data reload changed durations); Ice: `RemoveModifiers("Elemental.Frost")`; broadcast `OnStatusEnded`.

**Death and cleanup:** bind the owner's attribute `OnDeath` in BeginPlay -> `ClearAll()` (meters reset, Frost modifier removed, tick off). EndPlay: unbind and remove the Frost modifier. No timers survive the owner.

## 4. Damage path: `Combat/GBCombatLibrary.h/.cpp`
- `static int32 NewBuildUpEventId();` global counter, never returns 0.
- `static bool IsWeakPointHit(const AActor* Target, const FVector& HitLocation);` today: `AGBEnemyCharacter::IsHeadshotLocation`. The one place to add other weak points later; a head that is also a weak point is still one hit, so the bonus can never apply twice. Update the header comment.
- `ApplyWeakPointBonus`: `Info.bHeadshot = IsWeakPointHit(...)`; only if `bHeadshot && WeakPointMult > 1` multiply `Amount`. Returns `bHeadshot`. (Today all weapons have mult >= 1.5, so nothing visible changes; it decouples the build-up bonus from the damage bonus.)
- `static float ApplyBuildUp(AActor* Target, const FGBDamageInfo& Info);` returns the raw amount sent. Does nothing unless `Info.BuildUp > 0`, the element is Fire / Lightning / Ice, `CanDamage(Info.Instigator, Target)`, and the target has a `UGBElementalStatusComponent`. Amount = `BuildUp * (bHeadshot ? max(1, WeakPointBuildUpMult) : 1)`; calls `AddBuildUp(Element, Amount, BuildUpEventId, Instigator)`.
- `DealDamage`: right after a hit that dealt damage (`Dealt > 0`, where `OnDamageDealt` is broadcast), call `ApplyBuildUp(Target, FinalInfo)`. Leave the generic `OnHitEffect` code as it is (the ice floor's slow still uses on-hit effects).
- `Stats/GBAttributeComponent.cpp` `ApplyDamage`: multiply by the element's `<E>DamageTaken` stat (default 1) next to `DamageTaken`.

## 5. Sources

### Spells (`Spells/GBSpellTypes.cpp`, `GBSpellActor`, `GBSpell_Zone`)
- `FGBSpellCast::MakeDamageInfo`: `Info.BuildUp = Get(GBStat::BuildUp, 0)` and `Info.BuildUpEventId = UGBCombatLibrary::NewBuildUpEventId()`. Every behavior already builds its hits with it: a projectile gets one id for its whole flight (a lightning bolt passing through three enemies gives one build-up to each, never two to one; check `AGBProjectile::DamagedActors` still prevents a second hit on the same actor), an explosion or rune blast one id (each target in the radius once), and every tick of a wall, orb, flamethrower or zone builds a new info (one build-up per target per tick). Verify these call sites and say in the report if any of them builds the info differently.
- Flamethrower: build-up rides on its existing 0.3 s damage ticks (`AGBSpell_Beam::DamageTick` -> `DamageTarget`). Do not add another timer. Mana, QTE, hold-to-sustain and the strong-hit interrupt must stay exactly as they are.
- Ice floor deals no damage (`DamagePerSecond` 0) so `DealDamage` never runs: in `AGBSpell_Zone::ZoneTick`, the branch that only applies the on-hit effect also calls `UGBCombatLibrary::ApplyBuildUp(Target, Info)` with `Info = SpellCast.MakeDamageInfo(NAME_None, this)` (one info per tick, HitLocation = target location). Its slow, `EffectStrength`, Deep Freeze and the 80% cap are untouched.
- Ice wall and ice shield have no `BuildUp` param: they build up nothing.

### Weapons (`Weapons/GBHeldItem.cpp`, `GBGun.cpp`)
- `AGBHeldItem::MakeDamageInfo`: after the element is final (weapon element, or the gun enchant's element): if `GBElemental::ElementIndex(Info.Element) >= 0`, `Info.BuildUp = GetStat(GBElemental::WeaponBuildUp(i), 0)`; `Info.WeakPointBuildUpMult = GetStat(GBStat::WeakPointBuildUpMult, Data->GetGameValue("WeakPointBuildUpMult", 1))`; `Info.BuildUpEventId = NewBuildUpEventId()`. Use `GetStat` so `Weapon.*` modifiers reach these values. An unenchanted gun is Physical, the apprentice staff Arcane: no build-up. The sword uses its current gem element.
- Check that `MakeDamageInfo` runs once per trigger pull: `AGBGun` builds `BaseInfo` once per shot and passes it to `FireHitscan` / `FireProjectile`, so all pellets share one `BuildUpEventId` and the event log keeps only the best pellet (a head pellet after a body pellet adds only the difference). Same for the sword (one id per swing) and the staff (one per bolt). If any of them calls it per pellet, fix that.
- Weapon damage, falloff, the head damage bonus and everything else stay the same.

### Electric grenade (`Items/GBInventoryComponent.cpp`, throw path around `Config.Damage`)
- `Config.Damage.BuildUp = ReadItemStat(Def, GBStat::BuildUp, 0)` and a new event id. The grenade no longer uses an on-hit effect (data below).

## 6. Enemies (`AI/GBEnemyCharacter.h/.cpp`)
- Create a `UGBElementalStatusComponent` in the constructor (`ElementalStatus`, UPROPERTY VisibleAnywhere, getter `GetElementalStatus()`).
- BeginPlay: bind `ElementalStatus->OnStatusStarted` to `HandleElementalStatusStarted(EGBElement, FName Reaction, float Duration)`: Lightning `Stun` -> `ApplyControlLock(Duration)`; Lightning `Interrupt` -> `InterruptAttack()`; Ice `Freeze` -> `ApplyControlLock(Duration)`. Everything else: nothing here.
- `void InterruptAttack()`: if the state is Windup, `SetState(Recover)` (the telegraph goes off in SetState; the attack is lost and the interval starts again).
- `void ApplyControlLock(float Seconds)`: ignored when dead; `ControlLockTime = max(ControlLockTime, Seconds)`; `InterruptAttack()`; `StopMoving()` once.
- `Tick`: right after the dead / era-presence early return, if `ControlLockTime > 0`: subtract DeltaSeconds, `ResetStuck()`, and return **before** `StateTime` and the other timers advance (state timers pause while locked, so the attack interval runs after the lock). Gravity and knockback still work (character movement).
- `float GetControlLockTime() const` (for the debug view). Death sets it to 0.
